People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I came because everybody keeps acting like I’m the problem, like I’m making things up, but I’m not. My family and my friends keep waving bank papers around saying, “See, nothing was taken,” but those papers don’t prove anything. They can hide things in bills, and they talk about fundraisers and trips like that explains where the money went.

This has been going on for over a year, and I keep telling them I know they’re hiding my money. They won’t just tell the truth. And then they say I’m causing trouble because I keep asking, but what am I supposed to do, just let them get away with it? My cousin did that thing once with the trip plans, and everyone acted like it was normal, like I don’t understand papers or numbers, but I know when something is wrong. So… that’s why I’m here, I guess. They want someone to tell me to stop confronting them.

They can hide things in bills, and they talk about fundraisers and trips like that explains where the money went.\n\nThis has been going on for over a year, and I keep telling them I know they’re hiding my money. They won’t just tell the truth. And then they say I’m causing trouble because I keep asking, but what am I supposed to do, just let them get away with it? My cousin did that thing once with the trip plans, and everyone acted like it was normal, like I don’t understand papers or numbers, but I know when something is wrong. So… that’s why I’m here, I guess. They want someone to tell me to stop confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family and friends are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I mean… not like they all sit in a room with a chart or something, but they’re all saying the same thing, aren’t they? My family, my friends, everybody keeps pointing at those bank papers like that proves nothing was taken. But papers can be made to look right. Bills, fundraisers, trips—there’s always some explanation ready.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year of this. I keep asking because I know my money is being hidden, and then they act like I’m the one causing problems. My cousin did that thing with the trip plans too, and everyone just brushed it off, like I’m too stupid to understand. So yes, I think they’re covering for each other until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me… I don’t know if I’d put it like that, like someone coming after me physically. But they are hurting me by hiding my money and then acting like I’m crazy for seeing it. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but those papers don’t show everything.\n\nIt’s been over a year, and every time I ask, there’s some story about bills or fundraisers or trip plans. Like my cousin with those trip plans—everyone just acted like that was normal. So yes, in that way, they’re doing something to me, and I have to keep confronting them until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe before all this started, before I noticed the papers and the way everyone kept explaining things away. But for over a year now I’ve known something isn’t right. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers don’t show what people hide behind bills and fundraisers and those trip plans.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because my cousin—well, not funny, but you know what I mean—when those trip plans came up, everyone acted like I was supposed to just accept it. Like because it’s written down somewhere, that makes it true. So no, not lately. I keep confronting them because if I stop, then they just get to keep pretending, and nobody tells the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Doubts? Not really, no. I mean, people keep saying I should have doubts because the bank papers “prove” nothing was taken, but that’s exactly the thing—papers can be made to look right. I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.

Like, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And it’s not just one little thing, because then my cousin brings up those trip plans, and someone else says it was for bills, and then there’s some fundraiser story, and it all gets mixed together like they rehearsed it. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.

I keep confronting them because if I stop, then they just get to keep pretending, and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your belief that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Doubts? Not really, no. I mean, people keep saying I should have doubts because the bank papers “prove” nothing was taken, but that’s exactly the thing—papers can be made to look right. They keep pointing at bills and fundraisers and then the trip plans, like if they say it fast enough I’ll stop asking.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year, so it’s not like I just woke up one day and said this. I came in because they all keep acting like I’m making trouble, but I know they’re hiding my money. I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one person in particular who is in charge of hiding your money and organizing the others to cover it up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And it’s not just one little thing, because then my cousin brings up those trip plans, and someone else says it was for bills, and then there’s some fundraiser story, and it all gets mixed together like they rehearsed it. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think the explanations your family and friends give, such as the bank papers and trip plans, are reasonable or could there be another explanation that you haven't considered?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 28 / no_occupation / less_than_9th\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money and using bills, fundraisers, and trip plans to cover it up, so I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… reasonable to them, maybe, because they all say it the same way. “Look at the bank papers,” “it went to bills,” “there was a fundraiser,” “the trip plans explain it.” But that’s exactly why I came in, because they keep acting like those papers prove nothing was taken, and I’m supposed to just sit there and accept it.

I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. My aunt used to do that with grocery lists, she’d write things twice and then say she didn’t—sorry, that’s not the point, but it’s like that.

So no, I don’t think there’s some other explanation I haven’t considered. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. It’s been over a year, and I know they’re hiding my money.

They keep saying, “Look at the bank papers,” like that proves nothing was taken, but papers don’t settle it for me. Then somebody brings up bills, or a fundraiser, or those trip plans, and it all turns into this big mess. My cousin talks fast when she explains things too, like when we were kids and she’d try to get out of chores—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked.

But no, I haven’t really stopped believing it. That’s why I came in, because they keep acting like I’m the problem, and I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally tells the truth.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came because everybody keeps acting like I’m the problem, like I’m making things up, but I’m not. My family and my friends keep waving bank papers around saying, “See, nothing was taken,” but those papers don’t prove anything. They can hide things in bills, and they talk about fundraisers and trips like that explains where the money went.\n\nThis has been going on for over a year, and I keep telling them I know they’re hiding my money. They won’t just tell the truth. And then they say I’m causing trouble because I keep asking, but what am I supposed to do, just let them get away with it? My cousin did that thing once with the trip plans, and everyone acted like it was normal, like I don’t understand papers or numbers, but I know when something is wrong. So… that’s why I’m here, I guess. They want someone to tell me to stop confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family and friends are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I mean… not like they all sit in a room with a chart or something, but they’re all saying the same thing, aren’t they? My family, my friends, everybody keeps pointing at those bank papers like that proves nothing was taken. But papers can be made to look right. Bills, fundraisers, trips—there’s always some explanation ready.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year of this. I keep asking because I know my money is being hidden, and then they act like I’m the one causing problems. My cousin did that thing with the trip plans too, and everyone just brushed it off, like I’m too stupid to understand. So yes, I think they’re covering for each other until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me… I don’t know if I’d put it like that, like someone coming after me physically. But they are hurting me by hiding my money and then acting like I’m crazy for seeing it. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but those papers don’t show everything.\n\nIt’s been over a year, and every time I ask, there’s some story about bills or fundraisers or trip plans. Like my cousin with those trip plans—everyone just acted like that was normal. So yes, in that way, they’re doing something to me, and I have to keep confronting them until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe before all this started, before I noticed the papers and the way everyone kept explaining things away. But for over a year now I’ve known something isn’t right. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers don’t show what people hide behind bills and fundraisers and those trip plans.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because my cousin—well, not funny, but you know what I mean—when those trip plans came up, everyone acted like I was supposed to just accept it. Like because it’s written down somewhere, that makes it true. So no, not lately. I keep confronting them because if I stop, then they just get to keep pretending, and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your belief that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Doubts? Not really, no. I mean, people keep saying I should have doubts because the bank papers “prove” nothing was taken, but that’s exactly the thing—papers can be made to look right. They keep pointing at bills and fundraisers and then the trip plans, like if they say it fast enough I’ll stop asking.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year, so it’s not like I just woke up one day and said this. I came in because they all keep acting like I’m making trouble, but I know they’re hiding my money. I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one person in particular who is in charge of hiding your money and organizing the others to cover it up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And it’s not just one little thing, because then my cousin brings up those trip plans, and someone else says it was for bills, and then there’s some fundraiser story, and it all gets mixed together like they rehearsed it. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the explanations your family and friends give, such as the bank papers and trip plans, are reasonable or could there be another explanation that you haven't considered?\n\nPatient: I mean… reasonable to them, maybe, because they all say it the same way. “Look at the bank papers,” “it went to bills,” “there was a fundraiser,” “the trip plans explain it.” But that’s exactly why I came in, because they keep acting like those papers prove nothing was taken, and I’m supposed to just sit there and accept it.\n\nI’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. My aunt used to do that with grocery lists, she’d write things twice and then say she didn’t—sorry, that’s not the point, but it’s like that.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s some other explanation I haven’t considered. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mistaken? No… I don’t think so. That’s why I came in, because they keep telling me the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.

And then it’s always something else—bills, fundraisers, those trip plans. My cousin brings up the trip plans like that explains everything, and it reminds me of how people used to pass notes in school and pretend they didn’t—well, anyway. It just feels covered up. So I don’t think I’m mistaken. I have to keep confronting them until somebody tells the truth.

They won’t just tell the truth. And then they say I’m causing trouble because I keep asking, but what am I supposed to do, just let them get away with it? I keep asking because I know my money is being hidden, and then they act like I’m the one causing problems. My cousin did that thing with the trip plans too, and everyone just brushed it off, like I’m too stupid to understand. So yes, I think they’re covering for each other until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me… I don’t know if I’d put it like that, like someone coming after me physically. But they are hurting me by hiding my money and then acting like I’m crazy for seeing it. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but those papers don’t show everything.\n\nIt’s been over a year, and every time I ask, there’s some story about bills or fundraisers or trip plans. Like my cousin with those trip plans—everyone just acted like that was normal. So yes, in that way, they’re doing something to me, and I have to keep confronting them until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe before all this started, before I noticed the papers and the way everyone kept explaining things away. But for over a year now I’ve known something isn’t right. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers don’t show what people hide behind bills and fundraisers and those trip plans.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because my cousin—well, not funny, but you know what I mean—when those trip plans came up, everyone acted like I was supposed to just accept it. Like because it’s written down somewhere, that makes it true. So no, not lately. I keep confronting them because if I stop, then they just get to keep pretending, and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your belief that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Doubts? They keep pointing at bills and fundraisers and then the trip plans, like if they say it fast enough I’ll stop asking.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year, so it’s not like I just woke up one day and said this. I came in because they all keep acting like I’m making trouble, but I know they’re hiding my money. I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one person in particular who is in charge of hiding your money and organizing the others to cover it up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And it’s not just one little thing, because then my cousin brings up those trip plans, and someone else says it was for bills, and then there’s some fundraiser story, and it all gets mixed together like they rehearsed it. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the explanations your family and friends give, such as the bank papers and trip plans, are reasonable or could there be another explanation that you haven't considered?\n\nPatient: I mean… reasonable to them, maybe, because they all say it the same way. “Look at the bank papers,” “it went to bills,” “there was a fundraiser,” “the trip plans explain it.” But that’s exactly why I came in, because they keep acting like those papers prove nothing was taken, and I’m supposed to just sit there and accept it.\n\nI’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. My aunt used to do that with grocery lists, she’d write things twice and then say she didn’t—sorry, that’s not the point, but it’s like that.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s some other explanation I haven’t considered. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. That’s why I came in, because they keep acting like I’m the problem, and I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? I have to keep confronting them until somebody tells the truth.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think it's possible for you to change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 28 / no_occupation / less_than_9th\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money and using bills, fundraisers, and trip plans to cover it up, so I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t see how I could change my mind unless they actually tell the truth. They keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken, and it’s like everyone wants me to just nod along, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.

And then they bring in all these other things—bills, fundraisers, trip plans—like piling papers on a table makes it clean. My cousin does that thing where she points at one thing and then another, and it reminds me of when people would switch stories in school when the teacher came around… anyway.

So, no, not really. I came in because I’m tired of them acting like I’m supposed to believe the cover-up. I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally says what happened.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came because everybody keeps acting like I’m the problem, like I’m making things up, but I’m not. My family and my friends keep waving bank papers around saying, “See, nothing was taken,” but those papers don’t prove anything. They can hide things in bills, and they talk about fundraisers and trips like that explains where the money went.\n\nThis has been going on for over a year, and I keep telling them I know they’re hiding my money. They won’t just tell the truth. And then they say I’m causing trouble because I keep asking, but what am I supposed to do, just let them get away with it? My cousin did that thing once with the trip plans, and everyone acted like it was normal, like I don’t understand papers or numbers, but I know when something is wrong. So… that’s why I’m here, I guess. They want someone to tell me to stop confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family and friends are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I mean… not like they all sit in a room with a chart or something, but they’re all saying the same thing, aren’t they? My family, my friends, everybody keeps pointing at those bank papers like that proves nothing was taken. But papers can be made to look right. Bills, fundraisers, trips—there’s always some explanation ready.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year of this. I keep asking because I know my money is being hidden, and then they act like I’m the one causing problems. My cousin did that thing with the trip plans too, and everyone just brushed it off, like I’m too stupid to understand. So yes, I think they’re covering for each other until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me… I don’t know if I’d put it like that, like someone coming after me physically. But they are hurting me by hiding my money and then acting like I’m crazy for seeing it. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but those papers don’t show everything.\n\nIt’s been over a year, and every time I ask, there’s some story about bills or fundraisers or trip plans. Like my cousin with those trip plans—everyone just acted like that was normal. So yes, in that way, they’re doing something to me, and I have to keep confronting them until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe before all this started, before I noticed the papers and the way everyone kept explaining things away. But for over a year now I’ve known something isn’t right. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers don’t show what people hide behind bills and fundraisers and those trip plans.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because my cousin—well, not funny, but you know what I mean—when those trip plans came up, everyone acted like I was supposed to just accept it. Like because it’s written down somewhere, that makes it true. So no, not lately. I keep confronting them because if I stop, then they just get to keep pretending, and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your belief that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Doubts? Not really, no. I mean, people keep saying I should have doubts because the bank papers “prove” nothing was taken, but that’s exactly the thing—papers can be made to look right. They keep pointing at bills and fundraisers and then the trip plans, like if they say it fast enough I’ll stop asking.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year, so it’s not like I just woke up one day and said this. I came in because they all keep acting like I’m making trouble, but I know they’re hiding my money. I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one person in particular who is in charge of hiding your money and organizing the others to cover it up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And it’s not just one little thing, because then my cousin brings up those trip plans, and someone else says it was for bills, and then there’s some fundraiser story, and it all gets mixed together like they rehearsed it. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the explanations your family and friends give, such as the bank papers and trip plans, are reasonable or could there be another explanation that you haven't considered?\n\nPatient: I mean… reasonable to them, maybe, because they all say it the same way. “Look at the bank papers,” “it went to bills,” “there was a fundraiser,” “the trip plans explain it.” But that’s exactly why I came in, because they keep acting like those papers prove nothing was taken, and I’m supposed to just sit there and accept it.\n\nI’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. My aunt used to do that with grocery lists, she’d write things twice and then say she didn’t—sorry, that’s not the point, but it’s like that.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s some other explanation I haven’t considered. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. It’s been over a year, and I know they’re hiding my money.\n\nThey keep saying, “Look at the bank papers,” like that proves nothing was taken, but papers don’t settle it for me. Then somebody brings up bills, or a fundraiser, or those trip plans, and it all turns into this big mess. My cousin talks fast when she explains things too, like when we were kids and she’d try to get out of chores—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked.\n\nBut no, I haven’t really stopped believing it. That’s why I came in, because they keep acting like I’m the problem, and I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No… I don’t think so. That’s why I came in, because they keep telling me the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd then it’s always something else—bills, fundraisers, those trip plans. My cousin brings up the trip plans like that explains everything, and it reminds me of how people used to pass notes in school and pretend they didn’t—well, anyway. It just feels covered up. So I don’t think I’m mistaken. I have to keep confronting them until somebody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t see how I could change my mind unless they actually tell the truth. They keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken, and it’s like everyone wants me to just nod along, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd then they bring in all these other things—bills, fundraisers, trip plans—like piling papers on a table makes it clean. My cousin does that thing where she points at one thing and then another, and it reminds me of when people would switch stories in school when the teacher came around… anyway.\n\nSo, no, not really. I came in because I’m tired of them acting like I’m supposed to believe the cover-up. I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally says what happened.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think there could be any evidence that would make you change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 28 / no_occupation / less_than_9th\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money and using bills, fundraisers, and trip plans to cover it up, so I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Evidence? I mean… they keep saying the bank papers are the evidence, but those papers don’t prove anything to me. Not when for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money.

If they showed me something real—well, no, see, they always make it about bills, or the fundraiser money, or those trip plans, like if they talk fast enough I’ll get confused. My friend did that once when we were looking at receipts from a lunch thing, and everyone acted like I was making a big deal out of nothing… sorry.

I don’t think there’s evidence that would change my mind unless they finally tell the truth and say where the money is. That’s why I keep confronting them.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came because everybody keeps acting like I’m the problem, like I’m making things up, but I’m not. My family and my friends keep waving bank papers around saying, “See, nothing was taken,” but those papers don’t prove anything. They can hide things in bills, and they talk about fundraisers and trips like that explains where the money went.\n\nThis has been going on for over a year, and I keep telling them I know they’re hiding my money. They won’t just tell the truth. And then they say I’m causing trouble because I keep asking, but what am I supposed to do, just let them get away with it? My cousin did that thing once with the trip plans, and everyone acted like it was normal, like I don’t understand papers or numbers, but I know when something is wrong. So… that’s why I’m here, I guess. They want someone to tell me to stop confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family and friends are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I mean… not like they all sit in a room with a chart or something, but they’re all saying the same thing, aren’t they? My family, my friends, everybody keeps pointing at those bank papers like that proves nothing was taken. But papers can be made to look right. Bills, fundraisers, trips—there’s always some explanation ready.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year of this. I keep asking because I know my money is being hidden, and then they act like I’m the one causing problems. My cousin did that thing with the trip plans too, and everyone just brushed it off, like I’m too stupid to understand. So yes, I think they’re covering for each other until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me… I don’t know if I’d put it like that, like someone coming after me physically. But they are hurting me by hiding my money and then acting like I’m crazy for seeing it. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but those papers don’t show everything.\n\nIt’s been over a year, and every time I ask, there’s some story about bills or fundraisers or trip plans. Like my cousin with those trip plans—everyone just acted like that was normal. So yes, in that way, they’re doing something to me, and I have to keep confronting them until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe before all this started, before I noticed the papers and the way everyone kept explaining things away. But for over a year now I’ve known something isn’t right. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers don’t show what people hide behind bills and fundraisers and those trip plans.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because my cousin—well, not funny, but you know what I mean—when those trip plans came up, everyone acted like I was supposed to just accept it. Like because it’s written down somewhere, that makes it true. So no, not lately. I keep confronting them because if I stop, then they just get to keep pretending, and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your belief that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Doubts? Not really, no. I mean, people keep saying I should have doubts because the bank papers “prove” nothing was taken, but that’s exactly the thing—papers can be made to look right. They keep pointing at bills and fundraisers and then the trip plans, like if they say it fast enough I’ll stop asking.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year, so it’s not like I just woke up one day and said this. I came in because they all keep acting like I’m making trouble, but I know they’re hiding my money. I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one person in particular who is in charge of hiding your money and organizing the others to cover it up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And it’s not just one little thing, because then my cousin brings up those trip plans, and someone else says it was for bills, and then there’s some fundraiser story, and it all gets mixed together like they rehearsed it. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the explanations your family and friends give, such as the bank papers and trip plans, are reasonable or could there be another explanation that you haven't considered?\n\nPatient: I mean… reasonable to them, maybe, because they all say it the same way. “Look at the bank papers,” “it went to bills,” “there was a fundraiser,” “the trip plans explain it.” But that’s exactly why I came in, because they keep acting like those papers prove nothing was taken, and I’m supposed to just sit there and accept it.\n\nI’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. My aunt used to do that with grocery lists, she’d write things twice and then say she didn’t—sorry, that’s not the point, but it’s like that.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s some other explanation I haven’t considered. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. It’s been over a year, and I know they’re hiding my money.\n\nThey keep saying, “Look at the bank papers,” like that proves nothing was taken, but papers don’t settle it for me. Then somebody brings up bills, or a fundraiser, or those trip plans, and it all turns into this big mess. My cousin talks fast when she explains things too, like when we were kids and she’d try to get out of chores—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked.\n\nBut no, I haven’t really stopped believing it. That’s why I came in, because they keep acting like I’m the problem, and I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No… I don’t think so. That’s why I came in, because they keep telling me the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd then it’s always something else—bills, fundraisers, those trip plans. My cousin brings up the trip plans like that explains everything, and it reminds me of how people used to pass notes in school and pretend they didn’t—well, anyway. It just feels covered up. So I don’t think I’m mistaken. I have to keep confronting them until somebody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t see how I could change my mind unless they actually tell the truth. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No… I don’t think so. That’s why I came in, because they keep telling me the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd then it’s always something else—bills, fundraisers, those trip plans. My cousin brings up the trip plans like that explains everything, and it reminds me of how people used to pass notes in school and pretend they didn’t—well, anyway. It just feels covered up. So I don’t think I’m mistaken. I have to keep confronting them until somebody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t see how I could change my mind unless they actually tell the truth. They keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken, and it’s like everyone wants me to just nod along, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd then they bring in all these other things—bills, fundraisers, trip plans—like piling papers on a table makes it clean. My cousin does that thing where she points at one thing and then another, and it reminds me of when people would switch stories in school when the teacher came around… anyway.\n\nSo, no, not really. I came in because I’m tired of them acting like I’m supposed to believe the cover-up. I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally says what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there could be any evidence that would make you change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Evidence? I mean… they keep saying the bank papers are the evidence, but those papers don’t prove anything to me. Not when for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money.\n\nIf they showed me something real—well, no, see, they always make it about bills, or the fundraiser money, or those trip plans, like if they talk fast enough I’ll get confused. My friend did that once when we were looking at receipts from a lunch thing, and everyone acted like I was making a big deal out of nothing… sorry.\n\nI don’t think there’s evidence that would change my mind unless they finally tell the truth and say where the money is. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I can just stop thinking it, no. Not when I’ve known for over a year that they’re hiding my money. They keep waving those bank papers around like that proves nothing was taken, but it doesn’t feel like proof to me.

And then they bring up bills, or that fundraiser, or the trip plans—always some other thing to cover it over. My cousin did that with the trip stuff, and it reminded me of when people would shuffle papers in school so the teacher wouldn’t see what they were really passing around… anyway.

So no, I don’t think I can stop until they tell the truth. That’s why I came in. I have to keep confronting them, because if I stop, they’ll just keep hiding it.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came because everybody keeps acting like I’m the problem, like I’m making things up, but I’m not. My family and my friends keep waving bank papers around saying, “See, nothing was taken,” but those papers don’t prove anything. They can hide things in bills, and they talk about fundraisers and trips like that explains where the money went.\n\nThis has been going on for over a year, and I keep telling them I know they’re hiding my money. They won’t just tell the truth. And then they say I’m causing trouble because I keep asking, but what am I supposed to do, just let them get away with it? My cousin did that thing once with the trip plans, and everyone acted like it was normal, like I don’t understand papers or numbers, but I know when something is wrong. So… that’s why I’m here, I guess. They want someone to tell me to stop confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your family and friends are working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes, I mean… not like they all sit in a room with a chart or something, but they’re all saying the same thing, aren’t they? Bills, fundraisers, trips—there’s always some explanation ready.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year of this. I keep asking because I know my money is being hidden, and then they act like I’m the one causing problems. My cousin did that thing with the trip plans too, and everyone just brushed it off, like I’m too stupid to understand. So yes, I think they’re covering for each other until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me… I don’t know if I’d put it like that, like someone coming after me physically. So yes, in that way, they’re doing something to me, and I have to keep confronting them until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe before all this started, before I noticed the papers and the way everyone kept explaining things away. But for over a year now I’ve known something isn’t right. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers don’t show what people hide behind bills and fundraisers and those trip plans.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because my cousin—well, not funny, but you know what I mean—when those trip plans came up, everyone acted like I was supposed to just accept it. I keep confronting them because if I stop, then they just get to keep pretending, and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your belief that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Doubts? They keep pointing at bills and fundraisers and then the trip plans, like if they say it fast enough I’ll stop asking.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year, so it’s not like I just woke up one day and said this. I came in because they all keep acting like I’m making trouble, but I know they’re hiding my money. I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one person in particular who is in charge of hiding your money and organizing the others to cover it up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And it’s not just one little thing, because then my cousin brings up those trip plans, and someone else says it was for bills, and then there’s some fundraiser story, and it all gets mixed together like they rehearsed it. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the explanations your family and friends give, such as the bank papers and trip plans, are reasonable or could there be another explanation that you haven't considered?\n\nPatient: I mean… reasonable to them, maybe, because they all say it the same way. “Look at the bank papers,” “it went to bills,” “there was a fundraiser,” “the trip plans explain it.” But that’s exactly why I came in, because they keep acting like those papers prove nothing was taken, and I’m supposed to just sit there and accept it.\n\nI’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. It’s been over a year, and I know they’re hiding my money.\n\nThey keep saying, “Look at the bank papers,” like that proves nothing was taken, but papers don’t settle it for me. Then somebody brings up bills, or a fundraiser, or those trip plans, and it all turns into this big mess. My cousin talks fast when she explains things too, like when we were kids and she’d try to get out of chores—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked.\n\nBut no, I haven’t really stopped believing it. That’s why I came in, because they keep acting like I’m the problem, and I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? I came in because I’m tired of them acting like I’m supposed to believe the cover-up. I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally says what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there could be any evidence that would make you change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Evidence? I mean… they keep saying the bank papers are the evidence, but those papers don’t prove anything to me. Not when for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money.\n\nIf they showed me something real—well, no, see, they always make it about bills, or the fundraiser money, or those trip plans, like if they talk fast enough I’ll get confused. My friend did that once when we were looking at receipts from a lunch thing, and everyone acted like I was making a big deal out of nothing… sorry.\n\nI don’t think there’s evidence that would change my mind unless they finally tell the truth and say where the money is. That’s why I keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever be able to stop thinking that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can just stop thinking it, no. Not when I’ve known for over a year that they’re hiding my money. They keep waving those bank papers around like that proves nothing was taken, but it doesn’t feel like proof to me.\n\nAnd then they bring up bills, or that fundraiser, or the trip plans—always some other thing to cover it over. My cousin did that with the trip stuff, and it reminded me of when people would shuffle papers in school so the teacher wouldn’t see what they were really passing around… anyway.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I can stop until they tell the truth. That’s why I came in. I have to keep confronting them, because if I stop, they’ll just keep hiding it.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think there's anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Completely change my mind? I don’t… no, I don’t think there’s anything they could say unless it was the truth. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers can be made to look clean. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.

And it’s always the same—bills, that fundraiser, trip plans—like they throw all these other things in front of me so I’ll stop looking at the money. My family does that, my friends too. It’s like when someone changes the subject real fast and then acts like you’re the confused one.

So no, I came in because I’m tired of it. I have to keep confronting them until they finally tell me where it is.

So yes, I think they’re covering for each other until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Harm me… I don’t know if I’d put it like that, like someone coming after me physically. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers don’t show what people hide behind bills and fundraisers and those trip plans.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because my cousin—well, not funny, but you know what I mean—when those trip plans came up, everyone acted like I was supposed to just accept it. I came in because they all keep acting like I’m making trouble, but I know they’re hiding my money. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the explanations your family and friends give, such as the bank papers and trip plans, are reasonable or could there be another explanation that you haven't considered?\n\nPatient: I mean… reasonable to them, maybe, because they all say it the same way. “Look at the bank papers,” “it went to bills,” “there was a fundraiser,” “the trip plans explain it.” But that’s exactly why I came in, because they keep acting like those papers prove nothing was taken, and I’m supposed to just sit there and accept it.\n\nI’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. My aunt used to do that with grocery lists, she’d write things twice and then say she didn’t—sorry, that’s not the point, but it’s like that.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s some other explanation I haven’t considered. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. No… I don’t think so. That’s why I came in, because they keep telling me the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd then it’s always something else—bills, fundraisers, those trip plans. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don’t think it would change, not just because somebody says something. That’s the problem—they keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe those papers tell the whole thing.

I came in because for over a year I’ve known my family and friends are hiding my money. They keep bringing up bills, or the fundraiser, or the trip plans, like if they pile enough stuff on top of it I’ll stop asking. My aunt used to do that with mail on the kitchen table, just stack things until nobody could find what they needed—sorry, that’s not the point.

The point is, no, I don’t think it changes until they tell the truth. I have to keep confronting them until they admit where the money is.

I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one person in particular who is in charge of hiding your money and organizing the others to cover it up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. My aunt used to do that with grocery lists, she’d write things twice and then say she didn’t—sorry, that’s not the point, but it’s like that.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s some other explanation I haven’t considered. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. But they are hurting me by hiding my money and then acting like I’m crazy for seeing it. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but those papers don’t show everything.\n\nIt’s been over a year, and every time I ask, there’s some story about bills or fundraisers or trip plans. Like my cousin with those trip plans—everyone just acted like that was normal. So yes, in that way, they’re doing something to me, and I have to keep confronting them until someone finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe before all this started, before I noticed the papers and the way everyone kept explaining things away. But for over a year now I’ve known something isn’t right. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers don’t show what people hide behind bills and fundraisers and those trip plans.\n\nAnd it’s funny, because my cousin—well, not funny, but you know what I mean—when those trip plans came up, everyone acted like I was supposed to just accept it. Like because it’s written down somewhere, that makes it true. So no, not lately. I keep confronting them because if I stop, then they just get to keep pretending, and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubts about your belief that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Doubts? Not really, no. I mean, people keep saying I should have doubts because the bank papers “prove” nothing was taken, but that’s exactly the thing—papers can be made to look right. They keep pointing at bills and fundraisers and then the trip plans, like if they say it fast enough I’ll stop asking.\n\nAnd it’s been over a year, so it’s not like I just woke up one day and said this. I came in because they all keep acting like I’m making trouble, but I know they’re hiding my money. I keep having to confront them, even when it turns into a whole argument, because otherwise they just keep covering it up and nobody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one person in particular who is in charge of hiding your money and organizing the others to cover it up?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s one person, exactly. Sometimes it feels like one of them starts it and the others just go along, but then when I ask, everybody has the same kind of answer—bank papers, bills, fundraisers, trip plans, all that.\n\nLike, they keep saying the papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And it’s not just one little thing, because then my cousin brings up those trip plans, and someone else says it was for bills, and then there’s some fundraiser story, and it all gets mixed together like they rehearsed it. So maybe somebody is organizing it, but I can’t say for sure who. That’s why I keep confronting them, because sooner or later one of them has to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the explanations your family and friends give, such as the bank papers and trip plans, are reasonable or could there be another explanation that you haven't considered?\n\nPatient: I mean… reasonable to them, maybe, because they all say it the same way. “Look at the bank papers,” “it went to bills,” “there was a fundraiser,” “the trip plans explain it.” But that’s exactly why I came in, because they keep acting like those papers prove nothing was taken, and I’m supposed to just sit there and accept it.\n\nI’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money. And the trip thing, that bothered me, because my cousin brought it up like that was the end of the conversation, but it wasn’t. People can write things down and make it look clean. My aunt used to do that with grocery lists, she’d write things twice and then say she didn’t—sorry, that’s not the point, but it’s like that.\n\nSo no, I don’t think there’s some other explanation I haven’t considered. I think they’re using bills and fundraisers and trip plans to cover it up, and I keep confronting them because somebody has to finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe your family and friends were hiding your money?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, maybe people think I should have a moment like that, but I don’t. It’s been over a year, and I know they’re hiding my money.\n\nThey keep saying, “Look at the bank papers,” like that proves nothing was taken, but papers don’t settle it for me. Then somebody brings up bills, or a fundraiser, or those trip plans, and it all turns into this big mess. My cousin talks fast when she explains things too, like when we were kids and she’d try to get out of chores—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked.\n\nBut no, I haven’t really stopped believing it. That’s why I came in, because they keep acting like I’m the problem, and I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you could be mistaken about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No… I don’t think so. That’s why I came in, because they keep telling me the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd then it’s always something else—bills, fundraisers, those trip plans. My cousin brings up the trip plans like that explains everything, and it reminds me of how people used to pass notes in school and pretend they didn’t—well, anyway. It just feels covered up. So I don’t think I’m mistaken. I have to keep confronting them until somebody tells the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t see how I could change my mind unless they actually tell the truth. They keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken, and it’s like everyone wants me to just nod along, but I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd then they bring in all these other things—bills, fundraisers, trip plans—like piling papers on a table makes it clean. My cousin does that thing where she points at one thing and then another, and it reminds me of when people would switch stories in school when the teacher came around… anyway.\n\nSo, no, not really. I came in because I’m tired of them acting like I’m supposed to believe the cover-up. I have to keep confronting them until somebody finally says what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there could be any evidence that would make you change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Evidence? I mean… they keep saying the bank papers are the evidence, but those papers don’t prove anything to me. Not when for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money.\n\nIf they showed me something real—well, no, see, they always make it about bills, or the fundraiser money, or those trip plans, like if they talk fast enough I’ll get confused. My friend did that once when we were looking at receipts from a lunch thing, and everyone acted like I was making a big deal out of nothing… sorry.\n\nI don’t think there’s evidence that would change my mind unless they finally tell the truth and say where the money is. That’s why I keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you will ever be able to stop thinking that your family and friends are hiding your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can just stop thinking it, no. Not when I’ve known for over a year that they’re hiding my money. They keep waving those bank papers around like that proves nothing was taken, but it doesn’t feel like proof to me.\n\nAnd then they bring up bills, or that fundraiser, or the trip plans—always some other thing to cover it over. My cousin did that with the trip stuff, and it reminded me of when people would shuffle papers in school so the teacher wouldn’t see what they were really passing around… anyway.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I can stop until they tell the truth. That’s why I came in. I have to keep confronting them, because if I stop, they’ll just keep hiding it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen or anything someone could say that would make you completely change your mind about your family and friends hiding your money?\n\nPatient: Completely change my mind? I don’t… no, I don’t think there’s anything they could say unless it was the truth. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but papers can be made to look clean. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.\n\nAnd it’s always the same—bills, that fundraiser, trip plans—like they throw all these other things in front of me so I’ll stop looking at the money. My family does that, my friends too. It’s like when someone changes the subject real fast and then acts like you’re the confused one.\n\nSo no, I came in because I’m tired of it. I have to keep confronting them until they finally tell me where it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your family and friends are hiding your money could ever change, no matter what happens or what anyone says?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it would change, not just because somebody says something. That’s the problem—they keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I don’t believe those papers tell the whole thing.\n\nI came in because for over a year I’ve known my family and friends are hiding my money. They keep bringing up bills, or the fundraiser, or the trip plans, like if they pile enough stuff on top of it I’ll stop asking. My aunt used to do that with mail on the kitchen table, just stack things until nobody could find what they needed—sorry, that’s not the point.\n\nThe point is, no, I don’t think it changes until they tell the truth. I have to keep confronting them until they admit where the money is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. New information? I mean… if it was real information, not those bank papers they keep showing me. They keep saying that proves nothing was taken, but I don’t trust it. I’ve known for over a year they’re hiding my money.

They do this thing where they bring up bills, and then that fundraiser, and then trip plans, like all these little piles of excuses. My friend once did that with receipts in a grocery bag, just kept pulling them out like it meant something, and I remember thinking, nobody keeps that many receipts unless they’re trying to bury something… sorry.

So I don’t think “new information” would make me question it unless it was them finally telling the truth. That’s why I came in. I have to keep confronting them until they say where it is.
